Update
Chenbro  | Asia Pacific
September Sales +1% MoM/
+14% YoY
In this report, we focus on Chenbro's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September  sales were NT$2,476mn (+1% MoM/+14% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$7,395mn (-5% QoQ/+31% YoY), short of our 3Q26
estimate at NT$8,368mn (+7% QoQ/+48% YoY) and the consensus at NT
$8,552mn (+9% QoQ/+51% YoY).
Our view:
• 3Q26 revenue fell slightly short of the buyside expectation of roughly flat

QoQ, in our view, and 4Q26 should be flat to up slightly  QoQ.
• We think near-term sentiment will remain lackluster amid the temporary
hiccup resulting from supply-chain management issues and its customer's
model transition. That said, demand from its GPU/ASIC server clusters and
general servers should remain strong, driving  orders for its chassis and rack
offerings.
• Stay OW.

### Page 3

M
Update
Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chenbro (8210.TW)
Base case, derived from 27x 2027e P/E. We view our target multiple as justified by the 33%
earnings CAGR we see for 2025-28, which implies a PEG of 0.8. This is in line the average of
0.8 over 2014-24, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 12x for a 15% earnings
CAGR.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger AI capex outlook
n Larger market share gains within existing customers

n Faster penetration into new customers
n Higher entry barrier among peers that leads to higher ASP/profitability
n Better macro outlook
Risks to Downside
n Softer AI capex
n Less share gains within existing customers
n Slower penetration into new customers
n Intensifying competition leading to lower ASP/profitability
n Continuous macro uncertainties

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Allen Chang

Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C.
HTC Corp. (2498.TW): XR headset and AI glasses to support growth 
ahead; Near-term cost pressures remain; Sell
10 October 2026 | 12:24AM HKT
In September, HTC accelerated its global wearable expansion with the commercial 
launch of the new round-frame edition of its VIVE Eagle AI smart glasses alongside 
substantial software enhancements across North America, Europe, and Australia 
(news). Features include hands-free image capture, first-person POV live streaming, 
real-time AI translation, AI Notes, dashcam utility, and assistive vision for the visually 
impaired. Meanwhile, the company continues to anchor its VR headset strategy 
around commercial enterprise solutions, underscored by its flagship deployment 
with Axon which shifts hazardous tactical training into virtual environments to 
reduce operational costs, complemented by use cases in complex surgical 
simulation, interior space visualization, and virtual meeting rooms. 
While we maintain our constructive view on HTC’s long-term growth trajectory 
backed by sustained product innovation, we are Sell rated given near-term earnings 
headwinds - specifically core monetization drivers across XR/ VR/ AI glasses and 
software will require extended lead time to ramp adoption, compounded by 
consumer electronics demand softness and margin compression from elevated 
memory component costs. We would turn more positive if we see 
stronger-than-expected XR headset/ AI glasses growth driven by market demand, 
application development or customer expansion.  
Earnings revisions: We factor in 3Q26 revenues and revise down 2026-28E net 
income. We revise down 2026-28E revenues by 4%/ 0%/ 27% due to demand 
pressures of consumer electronics (impacted by soaring memory cost) and soft 
demand of XR products in the near-term. GM estimates are largely unchanged in 
2026-2027E, and we revise down our OPM estimates by 2.1/ 0.3ppts on a smaller 
business scale. GM are revised up by 5.7ppts in 2028E to reflect mix upgrade to VR 
hardware and software with high margin.

### Page 2

Valuation: With earnings revisions and an unchanged valuation methodology, our 12M 
target price is revised down to NT$32.8 (vs. previously at NT$34.4). We continue to 
derive our target price via 85% based on a fundamental valuation and 15% from a 
theoretical M&A valuation. (1) Our fundamental valuation is based on 2028E P/E of 
19.3x (unchanged), discounted back to 2026E at an 11.5% COE. (2) Our M&A valuation is 
based on 23.6x 2028E P/E (unchanged) then discounted back to 2026E at an 11.5% 
COE. 
Our target 2028E discounted P/E multiple of 19.3x is in line with XR peers’ historical avg. 
P/E at 19.3x in 2022 (method unchanged, Exhibit 5). HTC’s 2029 NI growth will be 15% 
YoY per our estimate, which is lower than XR peers’ (Meta/ Goertek/ Microsoft/ Apple/ 
Sony) 2023-24 avg. NI growth at 23% YoY. Our M&A target multiple is 23.6x 2028E P/E, 
which is derived from the peers’ historical avg. +1std P/E in 2022 (methodology 
unchanged). We use avg. +1std P/E of peers to reflect the M&A premium.  
Our 12M target price of NT$32.8 implies 4.1x 2026E P/S vs. 126% revenue YoY growth 
on average in 2026E-27E. The implied 2026E P/S of 4.1x is lower than the company’s 
current price implied 2026E P/S at 4.9x, showing that valuation is stretched. Maintain 
Sell.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - HTC Corp. 
Valuation methodology: We are Sell rated on HTC with a 12M target price of NT$32.8, 
which is derived 85% from a fundamental valuation and 15% from a theoretical M&A 
valuation. (1) Our fundamental valuation is based on 19.3x 2028E P/E, discounted back 
to 2026E at an 11.5% COE. The 19.3x target P/E multiple is derived from peers’ avg. P/E 
in 2022; (2) Our M&A valuation is based on 23.6x 2028E P/E and discounted back to 
2026E at an 11.5% COE. 
Key risks: 1) Better-than-expected VR demand; 2) lower-than-expected competition in 
VR headsets; and 3) lower-than-expected price pressure from peers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease 
to Rmb729mn (-10% MoM, +16% YoY)/NT$3,447mn (-10% MoM, +28% YoY) 
We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise down our 2026E earnings estimates by 1% to factor in actual 3Q26 revenue 
(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

### Page 3

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Genius Electronic Optical
Private placement with 3M expands its optical
connectivity opportunity
Genius approved a private placement with 3M
On 15th Oct, Genius's board approved a private placement to 3M UK Limited for
5.54mn shares at NT$869.6 per share, an 18.3% discount to the 14th Oct closing price
NT$1,065, representing NT$4.8bn investment and 4.68% stake in Genius. Proceeds
from the transaction will fund technology development, working capital, and bank loan

repayment. The two companies also announced a strategic partnership to develop
optical communication for AI data center and HPC. We believe this announcement is a
positive validation for Genius working with a tier one supplier in optical connectivity to
provide its precision components for advanced optics.
3M EBO technology could open up a US$100mn TAM for Genius
3M has been developing its EBO (expanded beam optical) technology since 2019 with
commercialization in late 2024 and deployment for Microsoft Azure data centers in July

2026. EBO is an upgrade to MPO connectors at the ends of fiber optics cables.  The
EBO is a lensed, non-contact ferrule that uses a prism lens to expand and collimate a
light beam to send across fiber before refocusing into a receiving fiber, improving dust
tolerance to cut cleaning steps and cable inspection times 85%. The technology was
supported by the MSA launched in May 2026, co-chaired by Oracle, with Meta, Senko
and Molex among the members for scale-out cabling. We saw opportunity opened for

Genius on the EBO ferrule, a small molded plastic part with a row of micro-lenses to
widen the light beam plus an anti reflection coating. This could open up a US$100mn
market TAM for Genius based on 4-12 ferrules per GPU (2-3 connections x 2-4 plugs per
connection) x 20mn accelerators x US$1 for the lens assembly.
Validation for its optical solution push with potential hyperscaler exposure
We believe the scale of placement at NT$4.8bn suggests scope to expand into CPO

where it could improve the MT ferrule used as the detachable plug end of the FAU to
hold the fiber in position when it is connected to a switch adapter or removable cable.
Genius  collaboration with 3M can bring the company closer to the end demand with its
direct partnership with Microsoft and bring revenue contribution forward to 2027,
ahead of Genius CPO ramp in 2028E. For CPO, we still see potential to reach 10% of the
TAM supplying MT ferrule, prism and V-groove.

Reiterate Buy with PT at NT$1,250
Our recent update on Genius had raised the PT from NT$700 to NT$1,250 reflecting the
company's potential CPO market with its progress sampling three precision components
inside the fiber array unit (FAU) targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.
The collaboration strengthens our confidence on Genius capability within the optical
connectivity industry, and reiterate our Buy on the compony.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model.
Genius' growth is highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by
volatility in handset demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their

camera design. Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of
new technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.

buying or selling an option, and for the complete risks relating to options, you must receive a copy of "Characteristics and Risks of Standardized Options". You may read the
document at  https://www.theocc.com/about/publications/character-risks.jsp  or ask your financial advisor for a copy.
Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.
Important Information About Sustainable Investing Strategies: Sustainable investing strategies aim to consider and incorporate environmental, social and governance
(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

萬海 (2615 TT)  Buy

近洋線進入需求旺季，給予投資建議為 Buy。  
投資建議 ：考量 4Q26 上旬美國線運價相對有撐，近洋線進入需
求旺季，目前 PBR 評價處於近年區間中下緣，給予萬海投資建
議為 Buy ，目標價 140 元。  
近洋線 4Q26 進入需求旺季 ：受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，
颱風因素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船
舶艘數、船舶周轉率下滑等因素影響之下，支撐美國線運價持
續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾
聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加所致。美國線方面，
10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆春
節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影
響而緊縮通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬，
通行等候天數增加進而降低美東線船舶周轉率，通航條件改善
後，將進一步減弱對美國線運價的支撐力道。近洋線方面， 4Q26
進入傳統需求旺季，運價有望延續 3Q26 漲勢。   
預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 19.84 元： 1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市
場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營收 336.41 億元，
毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營 業 利 益QoQ-7.86% ，
另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後
EPS 2.73 元。 2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收
QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認
列金融資產評價利益之下 ， 單季稅後獲利 115.35 億元 ， 稅後EPS 
4.11 元。 3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求
旺季，適逢氣候異常導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航
等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈
現上漲趨勢所致 。 預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億元 ， 稅後EPS 8.19
元。 4Q26 亞洲線進入需求旺季，價量有望齊揚；遠洋線進入需
求淡季，美國線運價走勢將較 3Q26 修正，預估單季稅後獲利
134.88 億元，稅後 EPS 4.81 元。整體而言， 2026 年供過於求壓

元， YoY+76.94% ，稅後 EPS 19.84 元。

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  公司 ESG 政策：  
(一 )企業對於因應氣候變遷或溫室氣體管理之策略  
1.萬海秉持對環境保護的承諾，除符合 IMO 國際公約規範之下，進行船隊
減排，減少空汙，並且落實  設備保養及改善，確保機器良好運作，以達達
到環保之要求，愛護地球，永續經營。  2. 萬海積極參與國際環保平台，透
過實際行動來推行節能減碳的環保策略：  (1)2015 年參與 Clean Cargo 
Working Group (CCWG) 組織，加強與同業分享環保知識、資訊、技  術的交
流，以推動節能減碳為目的。自 2017 年起依據 CCWG 新規範，萬海提供
CCWG 的船隊相關航  行、油耗數據須由驗證單位檢驗，萬海船隊已連續五
年 (2016 ~ 2021 年度 )排放數據均通過第三方  驗證單位 DNV 之驗證，此將
可主動吸引同為其會員的貨主、承攬業選擇使用符合其環保政策之航商。
(2) 持續參與世界航運協會 (World Shipping Council,WSC) ，針對各項航運事
務及環境保護議題，  進行討論。萬海為該協會會員，副董事長為該協會之
理事會成員。  (3)2019 年加入零排放聯盟 (Getting to ZeroCoalition) ，萬海
持續推動並實現國際航運減少碳  排放為宗旨，致力達成該聯盟目標。  
(二 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量目標  
依照 IMO 規定，以 2008 年為基準降低碳排率，  以 2021 年為止減少 30%
碳排放率，並預計於 2030 年可  以達到 50% 減排率目標，優於 IMO 要求；
未來將持續進行船隊汰舊換新計畫，並配以新型節能主機及  各式最新節能
設備，以達節能減碳、環境保護目標。  
(三 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量之預算與計畫  
1.預算：保護地球本為每一個企業之責任，本公司將以提升船舶性能，降低
船隊油耗，使用低硫燃  油，以降低溫室氣體排放，視該成本為必要性支出。
2. 船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan
(SEEMP) ：  依國際海事組織 (IMO) 之船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy 
Efficiency ManagementPlan, SEEMP) 規範訂定有效的能源效率管理制度，並
根據能源效率設計指標 (EnergyEfficient Design Index, EEDI) 作為新船能源
效率的標準，並據實揭露能源效率營運指標 (Energy Efficient Operational
Index, EEOI) 。  3. 萬海另依 IMO 法規要求制定 SEEMP PART II ，加入「燃
油消耗數據收集計畫」 (DataCollection Plan, DCP) ，藉由執行燃油消耗數據
的監測，管理和報告船舶於海上航行時所產生的二氧化碳排  放，並依法規
製作年度排放報告，經第三方查核認證後提交給國際海事組織。  
4Q26 近洋線進入需求旺季：  
3Q26 以來遠洋線走勢呈現分化。受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，颱風因
素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船舶艘數、船舶周轉率
下滑等因素疊加影響之下，支撐美國線運價持續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對
疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加
所致。  
美國線方面， 10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆
春節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影響而緊縮
通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬至 33 艘 (正常為 36 艘 )，

燃油價格處於高檔震盪：  
油價方面，近期新加坡低硫油價格持續回落。美伊戰爭前約 500 美元 /噸，
戰爭後一度上漲至 1,120 美元 /噸，近期則來到近 890 美元 /噸。以目前運價
水準而言，已完全轉嫁燃油價格變動對營運成本造成的影響。  
新船下水規劃：  
公司至 2025 年底船隊達 116 艘，運力共計 57.27 萬 TEU。 2026 年預計交付
6 艘船舶，包括 2 艘 7,000TEU 及 4 艘 8,700TEU 船舶， 2027 年預計交付 1
艘 8,700TEU 及 10 艘 16,000TEU 船舶。  
營收比重方面， 2025 年美國線比重約 23% 、中東印度線比重約 27% 、亞洲
近洋線比重約 37% 、南美線比重約 13% 。

1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營
收 336.41 億元，毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營業利益 QoQ-
7.86% ，另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後 EPS
2.73 元。  
2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收 QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營
業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認列金融資產評價利益之下，單季稅後獲利
115.35 億元，稅後 EPS 4.11 元。  
3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求旺季，適逢氣候異常
導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運

元，稅後 EPS 8.19 元。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 195: 9月物价数据解读：9月CPI、PPI同比涨幅均扩大

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月物价数据解读：9月CPI、PPI同比涨幅均扩大.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

市场研究部 2026年 10月 14日
事件
10 月 14 日，国家统计局公布，9 月 CPI 环比上涨 0.2%，同
比上涨 0.9%，PPI环比上涨 1.2%，同比上涨 5.0%。
解读
一、9 月 CPI 环比涨幅收窄，同比涨幅小幅扩大。
9 月 CPI 环比上涨 0.2%，比 8 月回落 0.2 个百分点，其中，
食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，与 8月持平；非食品价格环比上涨 0.2%，
比 8月回落 0.1个百分点；核心 CPI 环比持平，比 8月回落 0.1个
百分点；消费品价格环比上涨 0.5%，比 8 月回落 0.1 个百分点；
服务价格环比下降 0.2%，比 8 月回落 0.3 个百分点。9 月 CPI 环
比涨幅 收窄，主要原因是服务价格环比由涨转降。
9月消费价格同比比 8月小幅提高。9月 CPI同比上涨 0.9%，
比 8 月提高 0.1 个百分点，其中，食品价格同比下降 1.7%，降幅
比 8 月扩大 0.3 个百分点；非食品价格同比上涨 1.5%，比 8 月回

升 0.3 个百分点；核心 CPI 同比上涨 1.0%，与 8 月持平；消费品
价格同比上涨 1.0%，比 8 月提高 0.2 个百分点；服务价格同比上
涨 0.8%，与 8 月持平。9 月 CPI 同比小幅提高，主要原因是非食
品和消费品价格涨幅回升。
二、9 月 8大类消费价格环比 4涨 1平 3降，同比 6涨 2降。
环比看，8大类消费价格 4涨 1平 3降。
食品烟酒价格环比上涨 0.2%，比 8月回落 0.1个百分点；
衣着价格环比上涨 0.6%，比 8月提高 0.7个百分点；
交通通信价格环比上涨 1.0%，比 8月回落 0.7个百分点。
医疗保健价格环比上涨 0.2%，比 8月提高 0.2个百分点。
居住价格环比环比为 0，与 8月相同；
其他用品和服务环比下降 0.8%，比 8月回落 2.3个百分点；
教育文化价格环比下降 0.3%，比 8月回落 0.3个百分点；
生活用品及服务环比下降 0.1%，降幅比 8 月收窄 0.1 个百分
点。
宏观研究
9月 CPI、PPI同比涨幅均扩大

### Page 2

8 大类消费价格同比 6 涨 2 降，其中，交通通信价格同比涨幅
扩大。
交通通信价格同比上涨 4.3%，比 8月提高 1.8个百分点；
其他用品和服务价格同比上涨 5.9%，比 8 月回落 1.4 个百分
点；
医疗保健价格同比上涨 2.7%，与 8月持平；
衣着价格同比上涨 1.2%，比 8月回落 0.1个百分点；
教育文化娱乐价格同比上涨 1.4%，与 8月持平；
生活用品及服务价格同比上涨 0.2%，比 8 月回落 0.5 个百分
点；
食品烟酒价格同比下降 0.9%，降幅比 8月扩大 0.2个百分点；
居住价格同比下降 0.3%，降幅与 8月相同。
三、9 月多数食品价格环比上涨，同比季节性下降。
9月，鲜菜和鲜果价格环比波动较大。
鲜菜价格环比上涨 0.5%，比 8月回落 5.0个百分点；
鲜果价格环比上涨 0.6%，比 8月提高 3.1个百分点；
畜肉类价格环比上涨 1.4%，比 8月提高 0.5个百分点；其中，
猪肉价格环比上涨 1.2%，比 8 月回落 0.1 个百分点；牛肉价格环

比上涨 3.1%，比 8月提高 1.8个百分点；羊肉价格环比上涨 1.4%，
比 8月提高 1.1个百分点。
蛋类价格环比上涨 2.0%，与 8月相同；
水产品价格环比下降 0.8%，降幅比 8月收窄 0.1个百分点；
奶类价格环比下降 0.3%，降幅比 8月扩大 0.1个百分点；
9月，多数食品价格同比季节性下降，猪肉价格同比降幅较大，
蛋类价格同比涨幅较大。
畜肉类价格同比下降 3.7%，降幅比 8 月收窄 1.4 个百分点；
其中，猪肉价格同比下降 10.0%，降幅比 8月收窄 1.8个百分点；
牛肉价格同比上涨 7.5%，比 8 月提高 2.2 个百分点；羊肉价格同
比上涨 6.4%，比 8月提高 0.1个百分点。
鲜菜价格同比下降 8.1%，降幅比 8月扩大 5.3个百分点；
鲜果价格同比下降 1.5%，降幅比 8月扩大 1.0个百分点；
水产品价格同比上涨 0.4%，比 8月提高 1.0个百分点；
奶类价格同比下降 1.5%，降幅比 8月扩大 0.1个百分点；
蛋类价格同比上涨 14.3%，比 8月回落 0.7个百分点。

### Page 3

四、9 月油价上涨。
油价环比、同比均上涨。9月国际油价（布伦特原油价格）均
价 102.1美元/桶，比 8月均价（86.57美元/桶美元）上涨 17.94%。
国内交通工具用燃料价格环比上涨 4.5%，比 8 月回落 2.1 个百分
点，同比上涨 14.9%，比 8月提高 6.6个百分点。
旅游价格下降。9 月旅游价格环比下降 5.3%，降幅比 8 月扩
大 5.2个百分点，同比上涨 3.5%，比 8月提高 0.8个百分点。
五、9 月 PPI 环比涨幅扩大。
9 月 PPI 环比上涨 1.2%，比 8 月提高 0.8 个百分点，连续 2
个月上涨，其中，生产资料价格环比上涨 1.4%，比 8 月提高 1.0
个百分点，生活资料价格环比上涨 01%，比 8月回落 0.1个百分点。
在生产资料价格中，9 月采掘业 PPI 环比上涨 7.7%，比 8 月提高
5.0个百分点，原材料工业 PPI环比上涨 2.6%，比 8月提高 1.8个
百分点，加工工业 PPI环比上涨 0.2，比 8月提高 0.2个百分点。

其中，煤炭开采和洗选业 PPI 环比上涨 9.5%，比 8 月提高
6.7 个百分点，石油开采价格环比上涨 10.4%，比 8月提高 4.3 个
百分点，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业 PPI 环比上涨 8.4%，比 8
月提高 4.9 个百分点，有色金属矿采选业 PPI 环比上涨 1.6%，比
8月提高 2.5个百分点。
六、9 月 PPI 同比涨幅明显提高。
9 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.0%，涨幅比 8 月提高 1.2 个百分点。其
中，生产资料价格同比上涨 6.5%，比 8 月提高 1.5 个百分点，生
活资料价格同比下降 0.1%，降幅比 8月收窄 0.4个百分点。
在生产资料价格中，采掘业 PPI 同比上涨 25.4%，比 8 月提
高 7.6个百分点，原材料工业 PPI 同比上涨 9.4%，比 8月提高 2.7
个百分点，加工工业 PPI 同比上涨 3.4%，比 8 月提高 0.3 个百分
点。
其中，煤炭开采和洗选业 PPI 同比上涨 35.3%，比 8 月提高
8.7个百分点，

石油和天然气开采业上涨 24.5%，比 8月提高 14.0个百分点，
有色金属矿采选业同比上涨 21.4%，比 8月提高 0.3个百分点，
有色金属冶炼和压延加工业上涨 21.4%，比 8月提高 0.6个百
分点，
石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业上涨 21.6%，比 8 月提高 10.5 个
百分点，
化学原料和化学制品制造业上涨 12.1%，比 8月提高 3.0个百
分点。
化学纤维制造业上涨 12.3%，比 8月提高 2.7个百分点。

### Page 4

计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业 PPI 上涨 5.8%，比 8 月提
高 0.5个百分点。
燃气生产和供应业 PPI上涨 5.6%，比 8月提高 1.4个百分点。
9月，受输入性能源价格上涨和季节性等因素影响，CPI 环比
小幅上涨，同比平稳，PPI 环比、同比均涨幅扩大。预测 10 月
CPI、PPI均保持平稳。
投资建议
1：9月肉类、蛋类价格环比上涨，相关养殖机会增加。
2：9 月生产资料产品价格继续上涨，相关上游生产者盈利增
加。

## Document 210: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원

## Document 304: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 19 - 23

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ October 19 - 23.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23
Date:    1 hour ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 19 - 23
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation reports came in hot. September’s CPI rose 0.6%
m/m, lifting headline inflation from 3.4% y/y in August to 3.7%. Core CPI
rose 0.3% m/m, taking its y/y rate from 2.4% to 2.6%. Both topped
expectations. PPI final demand jumped 1.3% m/m. That pushed its y/y
rate to 6.3%, the highest since 2022. Retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, though
just 0.2% excluding gas stations.
Odds of a 25bps hike at the October 27-28 FOMC meeting fell from 22%

before the CPI report to 17%, despite the hot inflation data. Fed Chair
Kevin Warsh offered no signal on October's meeting at the IMF and World
Bank annual meetings in Bangkok on Friday. “I don’t prejudge meetings,”
he said. Odds of a December hike rose. We still expect the Fed to reverse
the remaining two of last year’s three 25bps “insurance” cuts. Fed officials
are now in their pre-meeting blackout period.
This week’s data calendar is light, with earnings season taking center

stage. Friday’s flash PMIs are the main US releases. The Fed’s Financial
Stability Report and Canada’s federal budget are tentatively due Tuesday.
Here’s more:
(1) ADP & Unemployment Claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a rise in the four-week average to 24,750 for the week ended
September 26, the highest since early June and up from a late-July
bottom of 8,250 (chart).
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 199,000, with the four-week

average at 198,500 (chart). Continuing claims fell to 1,710,000 for the

### Page 4

week ended October 2, matching their four-week average. The labor
market continues to improve.
(2) Flash PMIs. S&P Global’s flash M-PMI and NM-PMI for October (Fri)
follow September readings of 55.9 and 58.8 (chart). The services prices
component is worth watching after ISM’s NM-PMI prices-paid index
jumped to 70.7 in September, the highest since July 2022.
Flash PMIs for Australia and Japan (Thu) and for the Eurozone, Germany,
France, and the UK (Fri) also land this week. September’s M-PMIs were

52.9 for the Eurozone and 52.1 for the UK.
(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season picks up this week. Netflix,
GE Aerospace, and Northrop Grumman report on Tuesday. Tesla and
Meta, the first of the Magnificent-7 to report, follow on Wednesday along

### Page 5

with Lam Research, IBM, and Texas Instruments. Spotify, Bloom Energy,
Western Digital, Lockheed Martin, and Blackstone report on Thursday.
ASML and TSMC posted record Q3 results last week. ASML’s net sales
rose to €11.7 billion from €9.3 billion in Q2, with the gross margin up to
57.5% from 54.0%. TSMC’s revenue jumped 50.9% y/y. Analysts’
consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for S&P 500
companies of 24.0% y/y (chart).
(4) Global. China’s Q3 GDP (Mon) follows Q2’s 4.3% y/y growth (chart).

We expect a moderate pickup in activity. China’s unemployment rate for
September is also due, following August’s 5.2% rate.
UK CPI (Wed) for September follows August’s 3.1% y/y headline and
2.6% core (chart). Rising energy and input costs point to a further pickup,

### Page 6

with the input PPI up 8.2% y/y in Q2. Bank of England Governor Andrew
Bailey speaks later that day. UK labor market data (Tue) and PPI (Wed)
are also due.
Canada’s CPI (Mon) for September follows August’s 3.0% y/y headline
and 2.3% core (chart).

## Document 320: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

### Page 3

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

please conﬁrm your preferences. You can change your cookie
preferences here at any time.
Read more about our Cookie policy
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise
Cookie settings

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary:  Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid -term elections. The near -term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive , the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike:  Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm . Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far.  Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all -time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 1 8.2, still well 
under the 25 –30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September.  Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negativ e (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks?  We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25 –30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index  enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid -term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings:  
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable . We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike:  The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10 -year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near -term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10 -year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while  the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation  came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish . Unsurprisingly, the market -
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes  by June 
2027. A December rate hike  now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the  US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would  
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre -existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year -on-year (y/y) , but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline , supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge -off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities ’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute . However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate  from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq -100’s valuation de -rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although  its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category  with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agenc y rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12 -18 months, agenc ies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact . While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation  
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support . While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks  include rising deposit costs, mark -to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks  with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s  seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2 . Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures a nd improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break  above this level  would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US  consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia -Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire ’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated . We  maintain our  three- and 12 -month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure . 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure , with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September  at only around 60% of pre -
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

NZD/USD is likely to rebound towards near-term 
resistance at 0.57

## Document 402: The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Wrap on Asia_ Asia’s goods disinflation over_ (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
Mapping out Asia ...
Illiana Jain
Economist, Westpac Group
P: +61 403  908 032
E: illiana.jain@westpac.com.au
• For two decades, lower labour costs across Asia helped
suppress global goods inflation.
• Much of that disinflationary pulse originated in North Asia,

where production was increasingly offshored to lower-cost
labour markets.
• Today, ageing populations and rising incomes are lifting
labour costs across much of the region.
• While production continues to migrate towards South
and Southeast Asia, the next phase of manufacturing is
increasingly driven by capital, technology and power rather
than low-cost labour.
• As a result, labour costs are unlikely to provide the same
disinflationary impulse that supported goods prices over the

previous decade.
• Technology has historically provided a source of disinflation
as production costs fell and competition intensified.
• Asia sits at the centre of the AI supply chain through firms
such as TSMC, Samsung and SK Hynix, which produce
critical semiconductors and memory chips used in AI
infrastructure.
• Rising costs for electricity and critical minerals are increasing
production costs across the sector.
• Strong demand has allowed firms to pass these costs on

while maintaining healthy profitability. SK Hynix’s net profit
rose from ₩19.8tn to ₩42.9tn (+117%yr).
• Firms are also competing on performance and technological
capability rather than price, allowing technology-related
inflation to emerge for the first time in years.
• Many Asian economies remain reliant on imported energy,
leaving producers exposed to higher oil and LNG costs.
• Semiconductor manufacturing and the broader AI
infrastructure build-out are highly energy intensive.

Expansions by TSMC, Samsung and SK Hynix are increasing
electricity demand across North Asia while data-centre
investment adds a further source of demand.
• As technology firms compete with traditional manufacturers
for power, energy costs are becoming an increasingly
important part of the production process.
• Rising production costs are therefore being driven by both
higher energy prices and stronger underlying demand for
electricity.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WRAP ON ASIA
• Chinese producer-price deflation remains evident across
parts of traditional manufacturing, particularly sectors
facing persistent overcapacity such as automobiles
• Historically, Chinese producer prices acted as the pricing
anchor for much of Asia’s manufacturing sector.

• What has changed is that parts of the region are increasingly
specialising in industries where China is not the dominant
supplier.
• Strong demand for semiconductors, memory and AI-related
goods is allowing firms in Taiwan and South Korea to set
prices according to their own market conditions despite
ongoing weakness in Chinese producer prices.
• China’s deflationary pulse remains important, but it no
longer guarantees lower prices across the rest of Asia.

• Rising costs were previously offset by production shifting to
lower-cost economies elsewhere in Asia.
• Production continues to migrate towards Vietnam, India,
Indonesia, Malaysia and Thailand, helping contain costs.
• However, these economies are unlikely to replicate the scale
of China’s integration into global manufacturing during the
2000s.
• High-value production remains concentrated in North
Asia while firms increasingly value resilience, security and

specialisation alongside cost minimisation.
• Future supply-chain shifts may therefore help limit
cost pressures, but are unlikely to generate the same
disinflationary pulse seen over the last two decades.
• For much of the past decade, falling goods prices helped
offset elevated services inflation across developed markets.
The forces that drove that disinflation are now becoming
less powerful.
• While Chinese deflation and production relocation to lower-

cost economies will continue to provide some support, they
are unlikely to fully offset these pressures.
• As a result, imported goods may provide less assistance in
bringing inflation back to target than they have in the past.
• This leaves central banks increasingly reliant on containing
domestic inflation pressures, particularly in services.
• As a result, achieving inflation targets may require a longer
period of restrictive monetary policy than has historically

been the case.

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .
